WREN THE COW DIES we hear how much milk she gave. Bu we ought to have heard of it before. Pity to leave the good things we have to say about our loved ones until they are gone. Flowers on graves are right and seemly but it is better, we think,to give love-tokens to the living rather than the dead. The Cow gave good milk, and much of it, during a long life, but report said ncthing of it, and thought lees ; but at her decease, her virtues told out were so many 'twas enough to resurrect her. "My late wife," or "my poor dead husband," said with a semi- Sanctimonious whine, is enongh to make the poor creatures turn in their graves. LATEST NEWS. _ THE TRANSVAAL. SITUATION eI.)RE C.;OMPLICA [ED. Practically no information, except of a negative char- acter, was to be obtained from official sources yester- day. The attitude oi the Free State, it is generally con- sidered, complicates an already difficult situation, and though of course it wes net unforseen by Her Majesty's advisers that such a position might be taken up by Presi dent Steyn,it is beyond doubt that at the Cabinet Coun cil to-day the new phase will rec,ive careful consideration • Owing to the fact that there is every likelihood of the two Republics combining as enemies of Great Britain, President Steyn's message will probably result in the . Cabinet deciding on sanding se larger force to South Africa, the question of troops being one that will be considered to-day. It is anticipated in military circles, the same agency says, that the effect of the meeting of the Cabinet will be that Sir Redvers Buller will receive orders to leave ' for South Africa without delay. TROOPS ARRIVE AT KIMBERLEY, 2 GREAT ENTHUSIASM. KIMES RLEY, THURSDAY. The first detachment of the Reyal Lancashire Regi-- • mint arrived bore te-day, amid a scene of great enthusiasm. A large crowd had assembled at the station, and the troops were cheered again and again. ANOTHER THOUSAND TROOPS DESPATCHED. The transport Jelunga left Sou:hampton on Wednes-- day afternoon for the Medeerrause.n, having on board 1.047 men. 64 women, and 63 children, besides 65 racers and three ladies. The majority of these troops. • are prcceeding to Natal, via Gibraltar. 52,000 BOERS READY TO TAKE THE. FIELD. "GREAT BRITAIN WANTS THE COUNTRY." The following special telegram has been received at- the London office of the "Standard anu Diggers' News (a pro-Boer organ) : JOHANNESBURG, THURSDAY. The returns which have new come In from atl the field cerneta in the rrausvaal and Orange Free State show that 52,000 burghers are ready to take the field at short • notice. It is unfortunate that Sir A. Milner did net cable at the Blotmfontein Confer.erce a coxaplete pro-- gramme ef what Great Britain required from the Trans- vaal, because the Government of the latter is now under ; the impressien that however much they may grant some new demands will be sprung upon them. The Government of the Rep eblic, the members of the Yolks-- read, and the burghers generally have become impressed with the idea that Great Brita n wants the country, and that anything now granted wiil !iimply lead to further demands. DISGRACEFUL ASSAULT ON CYCLISTS. • At Chesham Petty Ses.siens two men named Witney and Chandler, were charged with assault. It was stated that Miss Irene Parrott and a gentleman friend, from London,were cycling through the village when they met four men,two of whom used diseraceful language to the cyclists. One of the defendants set a dog on thens,which pulled them from heir machines. The men were fined £2 each, while Witney and another man had to pay 5s each for bad language. ALLEGED DESECRATION OF CHURCHES 1 BY AMERIC&N TROOPS. WASHINGTON, THURSDAY. Mr. Root has cabled to General Otis to investigate e rumours of toe desecration by American forces of Catho- lie Churches in the Philippines in regard to which Cardinal Gibbons made representations to President M`Kinley. A MONSTRE TROUT. A brown trout, weighing 1011 b., has just been captured by Mr. A. Savage, a north country angler, while fishing at the meeting of the waters ef the Wyre- .e and Brock, in North Lancashire. The fish is 33in. long and 15in, in girth. Jo weight it is said to beat all records in the north. A POLICEMAN STABBED. • A Castleford (Yorkshire) constable named George , Drenham was on Wednesday night stabbed in the. abdomen by an unknown person who decamped. The condition of the policeman is dangerous. STOCKHOLM, WEDNESDAY. k TLIO " Aftonbladet "has received a telegram from the master of the Norwegian gutter Martha Lareask stating that on the 9th inst. ha found an anchor buoy marked "Andrea Polar Expedition "on the north coast of Bing Charles Island.—Reuter. DEATH OF AN EX-CHAMPION WALKER. The death is announaed at Stewartstown, Tyrone. on Wednesday, of Mr. C. B. Irwin, ex amateur Irish champion walker, and winner of Sandow's golden statuette offered for the best developed man in the United Kingdom. Mr. Irwin was well known politically across the Channel,haviug condeeted a series of Unionist campaigns in England and Scotland during the 1895 General Election.
